CVP "Send to VRU" and "Run Ext Script" Failures

# CVP "Send to VRU" and "Run Ext Script" Failures

### Download Options

Updated: September 23, 2013

Document ID: 116178

## Introduction

This document describes the "Send to VRU" and "Run Ext Script" failures. The approach taken is called Predictive Troubleshooting. With predictive troubleshooting, you will be made aware of the problem based on the logs, the related behavior, and how best to address it.

In Customer Voice Portal (CVP) deployments, call failures at the "Send to VRU" or "Run Ext Script" node are very common. Even though it is just a single node in Intelligent Contact Management (ICM), there are lots of signaling events that need to occur on CVP, the Voice XML (VXML) gateway and/or the VXML server. There are a number of places configuration must be completed correctly. Most failures happen due to misconfiguration or a failure outside of ICM. Once you are familiar with the most common configuration problems and/or failure on devices, it is easier to troubleshoot real problems in production networks.

## Network Diagrams

"Send to VRU" and/or "Run Ext Script" with CVP

## Background of "Send to VRU" or "Run Ext Script" Node

When a call arrives on ICM via the CVP, the Cisco Unified Communications Manager (CUCM) or any other Peripheral Gateway (PG), ICM might need Voice Response Unit (VRU) treatment to play announcements and collect user input. In order to do so, ICM needs to send the call to the network VRU first. Only the network VRU can play announcements and collect user input. So, if a call arrives from a Public Switched Telephony Network (PSTN) to ICM via the CVP or CUCM, ICM needs to use the "Send to VRU" node to send the call to a network VRU. There are some nodes which have implicit "Send to VRU", such as "Run Ext Script" or "Queue to Skill Group". Once the call arrives at the network VRU, ICM then executes the "Run External Script" node.

## How ICM Identifies Which VRU to Use

When ICM receives a call from the CVP routing client, and if the call needs to be sent to the network VRU, ICM looks for the customer instance configured for the dialed number. Once the customer instance is identified, ICM uses the network VRU configured for that customer instance. Then ICM finds the label for the routing client in that network VRU. Along with the Correlation ID, ICM sends the VRU label to CVP routing client. When a customer instance is not configured, the default network VRU must be configured. But as best practice, the customer instance should always be defined on the dialed number.

## Ladder Diagram

The call flow changes depend on where the call originates and reaches to ICM. It also depends on the signalling protocol used for the call. Based on that, here are the different ladder diagrams:

- Session Initiation Protocol (SIP) - PSTN Gateway sends the call to the CVP and the CVP sends to ICM

- SIP - CUCM sends the call to ICM and transfers to the CVP

SIP - PSTN Gateway Sends the Call to the CVP  and the CVP Sends to ICM

- The Ingress gateway sends an INVITE to the CVP.

- The CVP returns with 100 TRYING.

- The CVP determines if it is a new call or a VRU call. It checks the Digital Number Identification Service (DNIS) length. If the DNIS length is less than or equal to the "Max DIGIT" defined in the ICM subsystem, the CVP knows it is a new call.

- The CVP sends a NEW_CALL request to the ICM.

- The ICM router tries to match the route request DNIS with the dialed number.

- Once the dialed number is matched, ICM maps it to a call type.

- Once the call type is matched, it is mapped to a script. If the script is scheduled to run now, the ICM router executes the script.

- Once the script hits the "Send to VRU" or "Run Ext Script" node, ICM needs to send the call to the VRU. It tries to find the correct network VRU.

- Based on the dialed number, ICM finds which customer this dialed number belongs to.

- Once the customer is found, ICM finds which network VRU will be used.

- The ICM router picks the label defined for CVP RC and generates the correlation ID.

- The CVP gets the label 9999999999 and Correlation ID 10. It combines it and looks for a route. Since proxy is not used, it is a static route defined under SIP.

- The CVP sends the 999999999910 call to the VXML gateway.

- On the VXML gateway, 999999999910 matches a dial-peer which has bootstrap.tcl as the application to invoke.

- The VXML gateway accepts the call and sends 200 OK to the CVP.

- The CVP returns the 200 OK to the Ingress gateway with Secure Device Provisioning (SDP) copied from the VXML gateway's 200 OK.

- The Ingress gateway sends ACK to the CVP. The transaction of  the INBOUND leg of the call is now complete.

- The CVP sends ACK to the VXML gateway. There is a dependency here. If the INBOUND leg ACK is not received, the CVP does not send the ACK to the VXML gateway.

- In the meantime, on the VXML gateway the bootstrap application extracts the SIP headers to find out where this call came from. It uses this information to send  an HTTP request to the CVP.

- The VXML gateway sends an HTTP PING message to the same CVP where the SIP call came.

- The CVP Interactive Voice Response (IVR) subsystem responds with 200 OK.

- The VXML gateway now sends a NEW_CALL HTTP request to the CVP with DNIS=999999999910.

- Since the DNIS is great, the "max digit" is defined on the CVP (which is 10). The CVP knows it is a VRU returning call so it splits the label and correlation ID and sends a REQUEST INSTRUCTION to CVP with label = 9999999999, correlation ID=10.

- Based on the correlation ID=10, ICM knows which script on which node the call was waiting for. So it continues to execute the next step which is "Run Ext Script". It sends "RUN_SCRIPT_REQ" as PM, welcome along with other Error-Correcting Code (ECC) variables.

- With use of the ECC variables and the RUN_SCRIPT_REQ instruction, the CVP IVR subsystem builds the VXML document and sends back as 200 OK.

- The VXML gateway receives the 200 OK and determines which wave file to play and from where.

- It checks its cache, the file is not present, so it sends an HTTP request to the web server for the file.

- The web server sends 200 OK with the audio file.

- The VXML gateway sends the audio file as Routing Table Protocol (RTP) to the ingress gateway.

- Once the VXML gateway plays the file, it sends another HTTP request to CVP with CALL_RESULT, ERROR_CODE=0.

- CVP sends "RUN_SCRIPT_RESULT" to ICM with ERROR_CODE=0.

- ICM follows through the successful branch of the "Run Ext Script" node and goes to "Queue to Skill Group".

- Now, ICM needs to disconnect the VRU leg. So ICM sends disconnect on the VRU leg.

- At the same time, ICM sends the CONNECT label 2001 to the switch leg.

- CVP sends 200 OK to HTTP request of the VXML gateway to disconnect.

- At the same time, CVP sends BYE on the SIP leg to the VXML gateway.

- Once the BYE is sent to the VXML gateway, CVP sends a SIP INVITE to 91919191 which is routed to the VXML gateway as per the dial plan configuration.

- Once CVP receives 200 OK from the VXML gateway for the 91919191 call, it sends Re INVITE back to INBOUND leg/Ingress Gateway.

- The Ingress gateway sends 200 OK for the new reinvite.

- The CVP sends ACK to the Ingress gateway as well as the VXML gateway.

- The RTP path is established between the Ingress gateway and the VXML gateway and the customer can hear the ringback.

- Now CVP sends a SIP INVITE to CUCM for the 2001.

- CUCM sends a 100 TRYING and 180 RINGING event. CVP does not do anything with the 180 RINGING message.

- Once the agent answers the call, CUCM sends 200 OK.

- This triggers multiple events on CVP.

- CVP Sends BYE to 91919191 VXML ring-back leg. CVP sends a reinvite back to the Ingress gateway in order to change the audio path to Phone.

- The Ingress gateway sends 200 OK on the new reinvite.

- CVP sends ACK to Ingress Gateway as well as CUCM.

- Now, the audio is established between the Ingress gateway and the IP phone.

## Configuration

### PSTN Gateway Configuration

### CVP Configuration

The "Maximum Length of DNIS" must match the length of the VRU label.

CVP must have either a local route or proxy for the VRU label. If "Send to Originator" is configured, CVP uses the Ingress gateway as the VXML gateway.

### ICM Configuration

The dialed number must have an associated customer. It is recommended to have a customer associated for the call type as well.

The customer/ instance must be associated with Network VRU.

The CVP PG/Routing client must have a network VRU associated.

The Type 10 network VRU must be configured. All CVP PGs must have a label added. The label length must match the "MAX length of DNIS" configuration on the CVP.

The Network VRU script must be associated with the network VRU.

### VXML Gateway Configuration

Tool Command Language (TCL) applications must be configured. Appropriate applications must be associated with dial-peers. The "header-passing" must be configured. Cvphost must be configured in the bootstrap application.

## Troubleshooting

Since this document explains how to troubleshoot the "Send to VRU" and/or "Run Ext Script" problem, assume that the call from the PSTN is already in ICM, and ICM hits the "Send to VRU" node. The method to follow is to look into each step of the call flow that can go wrong and study the result and how it should look. These details are used in the lab.

```
IP Addressing: CVP - 10.66.68.169 VXML Gateway - 10.66.75.241 CUPS Proxy - 10.66.68.164 CUCM - 10.66.68.163 Label: Dialed Number - 2002 and 2003 VRU label for CVP - 1234567890 VRU label for CUCM - 9876543210 SIG Digits - 606 Debug: ICM - Route Request, Script Selects, Network VRU Tracing, Call Queuing CVP - Debug41 IOS - debug voip ccapi inout, debug ccsip message, debug voip application vxml, debug voip application tcl commands, debug http msg
```

### Issue 1: ICM Fails to Find the VRU Label

Analysis

ICM choses the network VRU based on the customer instance of the dialed number. If the dialed number has no customer instance, ICM fails to find any network VRU label for the call. ICM sends "RELEASE" to CVP.

#### No "Network VRU" Defined on Customer

Analysis

Once ICM finds a valid customer instance from the dialed number, it looks for the network VRU on that ICM instance. If no network VRU is defined, ICM fails to find the network VRU label. ICM sends "RELEASE" to CVP.

Analysis

Even if ICM finds a network VRU, the type must be 10 and there must be a label configured for the routing client from which the call arrived. ICM sends "RELEASE" to CVP.

#### PG has no "Network VRU" Configured


Analysis

Before a call is sent to the network VRU, ICM has to know if the Routing client has any network VRU capability or not. For this, the Routing client PG must have a network VRU configured which must be Type 10 (or Type 5 for the older style configuration). ICM sends "Dialog Failure" to the CVP.

### Issue 2: CVP has no Route for VRU Label

MsgBusMessageManager$4::processCallStateEvent: CALLGUID = F1482AAD1000013A7A23C37C0A4244A9, Call associated with message not found: ,, [MsgBus:CALL_STATE_EVENT],   ssId=SYS_SIP1,   eventId=CONNECT_FAILURE, causeCode=NONE, [id:2009]

Analysis

If all configurations on ICM are correct, ICM sends a VRU label and Correlation ID to the CVP. Now the CVP has to have a route to send the call to the VXML gateway. Either the CVP has to have a static route, or the CVP can use proxy to route the call. But in either case, the CVP must be able to route the call to the VXML gateway.

### Issue 3: VXML Gateway Fails to Invoke VRU TCL Script

#### Not Matching Correct Dial-Peer


Analysis

If the CVP is able route the call to the VXML gateway, the call must match the VRU dial-peer as the incoming dial-peer. If any other dial-peer is matched, then it will not have the required application for the proper treatment. In this case, it matches the incoming dial-peer  92001 and  CCAPI handles to application "Default". This happens when CCAPI on the gateway does not find any application on a dial-peer. Not matching the incoming dial-peer can happen for multiple reasons:

- No dial-peer configured for the VRU leg.

- The inbound matching pattern does not match the DNIS for the VRU call. Check for the number SIP message with the "incoming called-number" in the VRU dial-peer.

- Determine if the CVP prepends SigDigits which might not be configured in the VXML gateway dial-peer.

#### No Application/Service Configured on Dial-peer


Analysis

The VRU dial-peer in the VXML gateway must be configured for the VRU application which is bootstrap.tcl. If the dial-peer does not have any service/application configured, the VXML gateway tries to route the call and looks for the outbound dial-peer. If no outbound dial-peer is matched, the call fails in any case. In this case, even though it matches the incoming dial-peer 999 which is our VRU dial-peer, it still shows that the Call Control API (CCAPI) handles the call to the "Default" application. This means dial-peer 999 does not have the VRU/bootstrap application configured.

### Issue 4: VXML Gateway not Able to Send HTTP Request to CVP

#### Header Passing is Not Configured

<sip:1231234@10.66.68.169:5060;transport=tcp> Reason: Q.850;cause=16

Analysis

When the VXML gateway receives the VRU call, it matches with the dial-peer that has the VRU application configured. In order to find out from which CVP the call originated from , the VRU/bootstrap application needs to see the SIP headers. For this reason, header passing is required to be configured. In this case, the incoming dial-peer 999 is matched and CCAPI is handed off to the bootstrap application. When the application tries to parse the SIP header it fails. If the bootstrap application is not able to see the SIP header, it tries to send the HTTP request to the source address of the call. So, if the call comes via proxy, the HTTP message is sent to the proxy. In this example, 10.66.68.164 is CUPS proxy. Since the VRU leg of the call comes from proxy and no header passing is configured (no SIP header could be parsed), the application tries to send the HTTP request to 10.66.68.164 at port 8000. Now, proxy does not have port 8000 opened. So the TCP handshake fails and no HTTP message is sent. For proxy environment, either header passing is configured or "param cvphost" is configured to send the HTTP message.

### Issue 5: CVP Splits VRU Label and Correlation ID Wrong


Analysis

For the VRU call to work, the "MAX length of DNIS" and the VRU label length must match. When the HTTP request comes back from the VXML gateway with the VRU label and Correlation ID together, the CVP splits the VRU label and Correlation ID based on the "MAX length of DNIS". If it does not match with what ICM is configured for the VRU length, CVP splits the Correlation ID wrong, which is eventually rejected by ICM. In this case, ICM sends the VRU label  1234567890 and Correlation ID 8922. When the CVP sends the Request Instruction, it sends Correlation ID 922. As a result, ICM complains that 922 is an unknown Correlation ID. Since ICM did not get any response of call with Correlation ID 8922, it times out as well. ICM has no knowledge that the call with Correlation ID 8922 and 922 are part of same calls. So, for 922, it returns unknown and for 8922 it timed out.

### Issue 6: ICM/CVP sends wrong URL


Analysis

In order for audio prompts to be played for correct locations, all required variables must be set properly. There are few variables that contribute to the URL of the audio files. You need to ensure that whatever variable is set, the use of those CVPs creates the URL for the audio file. Audio files must be available in that location. It is also important to determine what kind of media server is in use. There is Tomcat for the VXML server which listens on port 7000, Tomcat for the Call server which listens on port 8000 as well as the Internet Information Server (IIS) which listens on port 80. In some cases, the customer might have another webserver which can be used as a media server. It is important that correct variables are set on the ICM in order to create a correct URL for the media files. The files needs to be in the correct location on the media server as well.

#### Media URL for VXML Server is Incorrect

close Accept: text/vxml, text/x-vxml, application/vxml, application/x-vxml, application/voicexml, application/x-voicexml, text/plain, text/html, audio/basic, audio/wav, multipart/form-data, application/octet-stream User-Agent: Cisco-IOS-C3825/15.1

Analysis

In ICM, the UsexVXMLParams Variable must be set to "N" if the VXML server is in the call flow. If it is not set, the CVP adds Server.vxml to the end of the URL which does not exist. As a result, the VXML server returns a 400 error message and the VXML gateway returns error code 9 which means wrong URL.

### Issue 7: SigDigit

#### CVP has no Route for the VRU Label after Prepending SigDigit


Analysis

When SigDigit is used, CVP prepends the SigDigit for all outgoing calls from the CVP before it looks for a route. If a static route is used, the CVP must have the static route of the VRU label prefixed with SigDigit. The same is true for the ringback number as well as Agent extension number routes. If a proxy is used instead, the proxy must have the route for the VRU label and ringback number as well as the agent extension with SigDigits prefixed.

### Issue 8: VXML Gateway Application is not Configured for SigDigit

FC13003B1000013A3987472D0A4244A9-1352848834619155@10.66.68.169 CSeq: 101 BYE Content-Length: 0

Analysis

If SigDigit is in use and the CVP has properly configured a route for it, the call arrives on the VXML gateway with DNIS as SigDigits + VRU label + Correlation ID. In addition to the dial-peer's "incoming called-number" which must match the above combination, the bootstrap also should be configured with SigDigit. If it is not configured, when the VXML gateway sends the New Call HTTP request to the CVP, it does not strip the digits, so the CVP sends the wrong correlation ID based on the "Max length of DNIS" to ICM. Therefore, the call eventually fails.

### Issue 9: CallManager gets the Wrong VRU Label to Transfer


Analysis

While the CVP PG must have the network VRU configured, the CUCM PG must not have the network VRU configured. If it is configured, ICM assumes that it can support correlation ID which CUCM does not. As a result, when the JTAPI Gateway (JGW) gets the message it only receives the VRU label, not the correlation ID appended. The call fails. However, if no network VRU is configured, the JGW gets the route as "VRU label + Correlation ID".

Analysis

If the CVP is configured for SigDigit, it strips a number of digits from the called number. When CUCM sends the call with "VRU label + Correlation ID", the CVP strips some digits from the beginning. The outcome is that the wrong correlation ID is sent to ICM. In this case,  ICM sends VRU label 9876543210 and Correlation ID 9026. CUCM sends the call to the CVP as 98765432109026. The CVP is configured for SigDigit = 3. The CVP takes out 987 as SigDigit. Then it splits the VRU label and Correlation ID based on the maximum length of DNIS which is 10. Out of 65432109026, the Correlation ID becomes 6 which is what CVP sends to ICM. And as expected, ICM returns unknown correlation ID 6, and times out for correlation ID 9026. In this case, the CUCM route pattern must prefix SigDigit (in this case 606) to the VRU label route to the CVP.

## Conclusion

It might seem difficult at times to troubleshoot "Send to VRU" or "Run Ext Script", but it can be easy if the right approach is followed. Due to dependency on multiple applications, it might require you to go through logs/traces from multiple devices. Here are the logical steps to follow:

- Start with the router logs. Determine if the router assigned a VRU level and/or VRU script to run.

- Check the CVP logs in order to determine how far into the message flow the call is.

These two steps give provide a very good understanding of the call status and lead you to the next step, which is whether to debug the Cisco IOS ® VXML gateway, VXML server, and so on.
